class="container"> <header class="title-section text-center"> <h2>How EOSDA Crop Monitoring turns satellite imagery into actionable NDVI</h2> </header> <div class="row row-cols-1 row-cols-sm-2 row-cols-lg-3 g-4 justify-content-center"> <div class="col d-flex"> <div class="card card-offer p-5 p-md-6 p-xl-7 text-primary"> <div class="icon mx-auto mb-5 bg-third radius-full lazy" style="" data-bg="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/offer-1.svg"> </div> <p class="h3 text-center"><span class="text-accent-secondary-1">Collects</span> gigabytes of raw data from several satellites daily</p> </div> </div> <div class="col d-flex"> <div class="card card-offer p-5 p-md-6 p-xl-7 text-primary"> <div class="icon mx-auto mb-5 bg-third radius-full lazy" style="" data-bg="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/offer-2.svg"> </div> <p class="h3 text-center"><span class="text-accent-secondary-1">Processes</span> imagery to detect and filter out clouded and shadowed</p> </div> </div> <div class="col d-flex"> <div class="card card-offer p-5 p-md-6 p-xl-7 text-primary"> <div class="icon mx-auto mb-5 bg-third radius-full lazy" style="" data-bg="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/offer-3.svg"> </div> <p class="h3 text-center"><span class="text-accent-secondary-1">Delivers</span> cloud-free NDVI-processed satellite imagery on the platform or app</p> </div> </div> </div> </div> </section> <section class="section-top-uses"> <div class="container"> <header class="title-section text-center"> <h2>NDVI Imagery in action: Top uses for agriculture</h2> <div class="row"> <div class="col-lg-8 offset-lg-2"> <p>Raw satellite imagery doesn't tell the whole story. Our analytical tools help you extract the meaning behind it — turning what you see into clear actions you can take.</p> </div> </div> </header> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center flex-lg-row-reverse"> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">Detecting and fixing crop issues early through NDVI change alerts</h3> <p>A wheat farmer in Kansas relied on NDVI satellite imagery to monitor crop health across multiple fields throughout the growing season. When EOSDA Crop Monitoring <span class="text-accent-primary-1">flagged a sudden index drop</span> in one field area, he inspected the area and found a local problem affecting wheat growth. Immediate treatment stopped the problem from spreading, protecting the yield. Without the alert from <a href="https://eos.com/products/crop-monitoring/precision-agriculture-software/">precision agriculture software</a>, the damage could have gone unnoticed until it was too late to rectify.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>With NDVI change alerts, the farmer <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">detected crop stress early</span> and <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">preserved both yield quantity and grain quality</span> — avoiding costly crop losses.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-uses-1.png" alt="area with the rapid change in crop health"> </div> </div> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center "> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">Identifying low-productivity areas with year-to-year NDVI comparisons</h3> <p>Consistently low index readings over several growing seasons often point to problems like poor drainage, unsuitable soil pH, low nutrient levels, or soil compaction. Reviewing NDVI imagery from past seasons allows farmers to pinpoint underperforming areas and refine their management strategies for the next seasons.</p> <p>That's exactly what an Iowa corn farmer did. He monitored NDVI satellite imagery over the years and noticed that some field areas were consistently underperforming. Past satellite imagery and field data helped him recognize <span class="text-accent-primary-1">poor drainage</span> and <span class="text-accent-primary-1">soil compaction</span> as the root causes. The farmer made several adjustments to his strategy, such as tilling the soil deeply and enhancing drainage. The following season, corn began to grow and produce far better, as reflected by increased index values.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>By using NDVI imagery to monitor seasonal crop development, the farmer made everything in his power to <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">turn underperforming areas into productive ones</span> while optimizing resource allocation across all fields.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-uses-2.png" alt="year-to-year productivity comparison"> </div> </div> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center flex-lg-row-reverse"> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">Precision nitrogen management based on NDVI imagery</h3> <p>A Thai corn farmer transformed his fertilization strategy using <span class="text-accent-primary-1">NDVI satellite imagery</span>. Mid-season, the imagery analytics revealed distinct <span class="text-accent-primary-1">zones of crop stress</span> that conventional observation missed. Soil analysis confirmed nitrogen deficiency in precisely the areas showing poor index values. Armed with this knowledge, the farmer applied fertilizer treatments only to affected field areas.</p> <p>The plants' response was striking — they recovered within weeks, so index values normalized too. NDVI imagery analysis shows the best results for nitrogen-demanding crops like corn, wheat, and rice, where chlorophyll levels directly reflect nitrogen status. We recommend pairing NDVI with ReCI (Red Chlorophyll Index) to get a complete and precise nitrogen profile.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>With NDVI satellite insights being a part of his fertilization strategy, the farmer <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">optimized nitrogen application</span>, achieving healthier, more <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">uniform crop growth</span> and increased harvest quantity and quality.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-uses-3.png" alt="fields before and after nitrogen management"> </div> </div> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center "> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">NDVI-powered variable rate input application</h3> <p>NDVI imagery allows agriculture producers to apply fertilizer where it will actually work. Through detailed imagery analysis, farmers distinguish between high-vigor areas with strong growth potential and low-potential areas with limited response capacity. Rather than treating fields uniformly, NDVI-guided application concentrates fertilizer in high-response zones while reducing waste in areas where additional inputs won't translate to yield.</p> <p>A Brazilian soybean producer carried out this precision agriculture approach, using NDVI imagery to categorize his fields into distinct productivity zones. His revised strategy: <span class="text-accent-primary-1">minimal fertilizing for low-potential zones</span> with poor uptake capacity and <span class="text-accent-primary-1">targeted enrichment</span> of high-vigor areas primed for maximum response. This new approach, based on NDVI maps for download into agricultural machinery, boosted soybean productivity in fertilized areas and eliminated the waste inherent in the traditional blanket application.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>Switching to NDVI-powered <a href="https://eos.com/products/crop-monitoring/vra-maps/">variable rate fertilizer application</a> brought dual rewards: <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">higher crop productivity</span> today and more sustainable practices that make better use of resources and <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">improve fields' health year after year</span>.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-uses-4.png" alt="NDVI mapping on EOSDA Crop Monitoring"> </div> </div> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center flex-lg-row-reverse"> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">Desiccation planning with the help of NDVI imagery</h3> <p>One of the greatest challenges for our large-scale client was that crops didn't all ripen at the same time: wait for everything to ripen, and you <span class="text-accent-primary-1">risk weather damage</span>; harvest too early, and <span class="text-accent-primary-1">quality suffers</span>. However, judging where to use desiccants by eye often led to mistakes.</p> <p>NDVI agriculture field mapping changed this. Now, the crop producer sees on satellite imagery exactly <span class="text-accent-primary-1">which parts of their fields are behind the maturation schedule</span> and need drying agents. They spray only those areas, getting all crops ready for harvesting at the same time. This means one efficient pass with the combine instead of multiple partial harvests: equipment runs fewer hours, burning less fuel, and workers spend less time in the field. Grain comes out cleaner with less green material mixed in. And because the crop producer is spraying chemicals only where needed, they reduce their environmental footprint.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>Thanks to NDVI imagery, the crop producer <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">moved from a multi-phase to a single-pass harvest process</span>, delivering higher-quality produce.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-user-5_ndvi_en.png" alt="planning and results of precision desiccation driven by NDVI imagery"> </div> </div> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center "> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">Cost-efficient plant growth regulation with NDVI mapping</h3> <p>A Ukrainian wheat farmer battled growing condition variability that created unpredictable growth patterns across his fields. Traditional uniform plant growth regulator (PGR) application proved inefficient — simultaneously <span class="text-accent-primary-1">wasting product on underdeveloped plants and insufficiently treating overgrown ones</span>.</p> <p>NDVI imagery revealed the exact wheat growth zones, which let the farmer implement zone-specific PGR rates: higher doses for areas with strong growth that tend to lodge, lower doses for areas with moderate growth, and no application for areas that are struggling. Our platform's algorithms transformed NDVI imagery into maps for download and use on agriculture machinery to apply plant growth regulators precisely. This not only synchronized plant development and yield consistency throughout the field but also eliminated wasteful and harmful over-application.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>With NDVI crop monitoring, the wheat grower achieved the most <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">consistent crop maturity and harvest quality</span> in his farming history while <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">reducing PGR costs</span> and chemical load on the environment.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-uses-6.png" alt="vegetation before and after growth regulator application"> </div> </div> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center flex-lg-row-reverse"> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">Spotting crop growth delays using NDVI imagery</h3> <p>An Iowa corn farmer caught a serious problem early using remote sensing and NDVI analysis. His field showed an average reading of 0.25, while EOSDA Crop Monitoring indicated that healthy corn should measure between 0.35 and 0.4 at that growth stage. Currently, the platform provides typical NDVI ranges for 20 common cash crops, reflecting average vegetation according to crop type and growth stage. Not taking chances, the farmer conducted a manual field inspection to verify satellite imagery warnings.</p> <p>His on-the-ground inspection confirmed what the imagery signaled — <span class="text-accent-primary-1">plants were developing more slowly due to insufficient nutrients</span>. So he quickly changed his field management approach, applying treatments to struggling areas while maintaining his standard program for healthy ones.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>NDVI imagery helped the agriculture producer <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">detect and address the specific nutrient deficiency early</span>, allowing them to recover corn's healthy growth pattern and protect potential yields that might otherwise have been damaged.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-uses-7.png" alt="spot of NDVI drop"> </div> </div> <div class="row mt-7 align-items-center "> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0"> <div class="bg-secondary text-primary radius-md p-4 p-md-5 p-lg-6"> <h3 class="h4 text-accent-secondary-1">Detecting areas of crop stress through NDVI monitoring</h3> <p>When a hailstorm hit his farm, a German farmer turned to NDVI software to see the real damage rather than rely on estimates. Index monitoring showed a clear signature of damage — <span class="text-accent-primary-1">a sudden drop in vegetation health</span> in specific field areas. Using Split View comparison, the farmer positioned pre-storm imagery alongside post-storm imagery, revealing the exact boundaries of the affected area.</p> <p>Damage assessment based on satellite imagery provided clear documentation for insurance purposes while enabling targeted recovery efforts in the hard-hit areas. Beyond this single weather event, his consistent satellite imagery monitoring helped detect other stress factors throughout the growing season. During dry spells, he noted how index patterns changed as drought stress developed. To confirm drought as the specific cause rather than disease or nutrient deficiencies, he incorporated Normalized Difference Moisture Index (NDMI) analysis — the index focused specifically on plant moisture content instead of general vegetation health.</p> <div class="bg-medium p-3 p-md-4 p-lg-5 mt-4"> <p>NDVI imagery comparison revealed precise hail damage boundaries, <span class="text-accent-secondary-1">saving time on field assessment</span> while providing solid evidence for insurance claims and guiding targeted recovery actions.</p> </div> </div> </div> <div class="col-md-10 col-lg-6 offset-md-1 offset-lg-0 mt-4 mt-lg-0"> <img class="fluid lazy" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201%201'%3E%3C/svg%3E" data-src="https://eos.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/top-uses-8.png" alt="hail consequences evaluation with NDVI imagery"> </div> </div> </div> </section> <section class="section-banner bg-third">
